### Make a distinction between touching and updating the lock manifest

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2015-09-17 12:40:44 +0200
**Commit:** 25b813c4e62520ab60a903c11e464c17e34ffcf0

Previously, deps.compile would update the parent project
lock manifest, which updated its Elixir version and made
it impossible to detect later on that we were upgrading
from a previous Elixir version.

This commit adds the ability to both touch and update
the manifest file and change deps.compile to only touch
it.

### Add a couple of tests for assert_raise/3

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-09-13 11:48:01 +0200
**Commit:** cd9253d1027bf8c0b0d34cb6ccd98f1a7b872d3e

### Use multiline failure message for asser_raise/3

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-09-13 11:37:51 +0200
**Commit:** db93099120fdaad018d7d90c6e197fa9c0ba65f2

Before this commit, `assert_raise/3` in ExUnit used an uncomfortable
failure message when the expected exception message and the real one
didn't match: they were both written on the same line, which caused
problems for long-ish messages.

Now, each exception message is printed on its own line, which should
make it clearer to spot differences and cleaner in general.

----

### Fix bugs involving Markdown code

**Author:** eksperimental <eksperimental@users.noreply.github.com>
**Date:** 2015-09-11 15:06:52 +0700
**Commit:** eca59aad1700526f4ece1f839aadd5455880fd47

Fix common mistakes like the random backtick hanging in the wrong place,
or the wrongly formatted link, or single-quotes instead of backtick.

Commands to detect these common mistakes:
        ## COMMON MISTAKES
        # single backtick in html docs
        ag '`' ./doc --ignore "*.css" --ignore "*.js"
        # links in the wrong format
        ag '\[\w+://[^)\] ]+\]'
        ag '\(\w+://[^)\] ]+\]'
        ag '\[\w+://[^)\] ]+\)'
        # find html code like: ["Strictly Pretty" (2000) by Christian Lindig][0]</p>
        ag '\[[^\]]+\](\(|\[)' doc/ --ignore "*.js"

----

### Standardize use of backticks and quotes; including Error messages and Title Case commands

**Author:** eksperimental <eksperimental@users.noreply.github.com>
**Date:** 2015-09-09 12:35:17 +0700
**Commit:** ed5671b4f8c44581d1b0e472196903bbc7452655

This is a large commit that does the following:
* Removes backticks from messages and comments.
  All references to backticks have been replaced with double quotes everywhere the
  code is not interpreted as Markdown, (ie. anywhere outside documentation and
  markdown files, such as in code comments, or error messages).

* Variables in Exception messages are printed using `inspect`
  The way no-matching error message are printed, have changed because now we use inspect for printing
  variables. The following file and their respective test have been changed:
  - lib/elixir/lib/exception.ex
  - lib/elixir/lib/inspect/algebra.ex
  - lib/elixir/test/elixir/inspect_test.exs
  - lib/ex_unit/test/ex_unit/formatter_test.exs
  - lib/elixir/test/elixir/exception_test.exs

* Properly use Title Case for Mix, Git, Dializer

* Use backticks when citing a command

****************************************************
CONVENTION FOR RENAMING USING BACKTICKS AND QUOTES

https://github.com/elixir-lang/elixir/pull/3697#issuecomment-138811747

1. Backticks should never be printed in error messages, neither be included anywhere where Markdown code is not interpreted as such.

2. Do not use single quotes anywhere. We should favor double quotes everywhere, to avoid confusion

3. If you want to format something in error messages, use inspect. For example, if you want to show the dependency name and that is an atom, instead of the dependency "foo", let's show the dependency :foo. Less noise and may click better

4. Similarly, if you want to show something with double quotes, call inspect, as it handles escaping as well as the quotes

5. Things like "--all" just add verbosity, we can definitely read --all without ambiguity.

6. When using switches with a single hyphen (such as "-o"), we can make it explicit in the text: e.g. "... give the switch -o when choosing ..."

****************************************************
SHELL COMMANDS TO DETECT CODE BREAKING THE RULES

* Detect values surrounded by single-quotes where we want double-quotes.

    # regular variables
    ag "'#{"
    # constants or ENV variables
    ag "'[A-Z][A-Z0-9_-]+"
    # switches
    ag -i "(?<!(\[))'--?[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]+'"
    # atoms
    ag -i "(?<!(\[))':[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]+'"

    # DETECT BACKTICKS OUTSIDE DOCS
    ag "^\s+(?<!(#))#[^#\r\n]*\`" --ignore "*.md"
    ag '^\s+["%].*`' --ignore "*.md"
    ag -s '(raise|Error)\b(?!(`|/)).*\`'

* Spot mentions to running a command that is not using backticks:

    commands="elixir|elixirc|mix|iex|ex_doc|git|make|rebar|dialyzer|erl|rm|cd|mkdir|rmdir|ln|ls|pwd"
    actions="runs?|running|executes?|executing|types?|typing|enters?|entering|calls?|calling"
    ag '(?i)('${actions}')(?-i)\b[^`\r\n/]+[\ \t]+(?!(`))('${commands}')'
    ag '(?-i)\b(?!(`))('${commands}')[\ \t]+[^`\r\n/]+(?i)(commands?)'

* Spot where a command is mentioned:

    #commands="erlang|elixir|eex|iex|ex_unit|ex_doc|logger|elixirc|git|make|rebar|dialyzer|erl|rm|cd|mkdir|rmdir|ln|ls|pwd"
    commands="mix|elixirc|git|make|rebar|dialyzer|erl|rm|cd|mkdir|rmdir|ln|ls|pwd"
    ag -s '(?<!(\`))(?<!(\.))(?<!(/))(?<!(:))(?<!(_))\b('${commands}')\b(?!(:))(?!(\?))(?!(_))(?!(-))(?!(\.))(?!(\`))(?!(/))(?!(>))' \
    --ignore "*.erl" --ignore "*.yrl" --ignore "*.src"

----

  ag "(@(module|short)?doc)\s+(~[Ss])?([\"']{3})(\r|\n|.)\s*+((?!((?i)${ignore_words}(?-i)))[\w'-]+)(?<!(s))(?<!(ed))(?<!(ity))(?<!(ally))(?<!(ily))(?<!(ely))(?=[\s\r\n,:.])"

### Add text to all links

**Author:** eksperimental <eksperimental@users.noreply.github.com>
**Date:** 2015-09-08 23:02:23 +0700
**Commit:** 06d94f5b90c2ddefbfb445cad5634a9829b897c2

This commits covers 100% of all links in the documentation,
using a words to describe what the page is about.

It users the `:module.function/arity` format for erlang functions that
link to the earlang docs.

I also changed some of the sentnces using the link from
- See [:erlang.function/2](http://...) for a list of available options.

to
- For a list of available options, see [:erlang.function/2](http://...)

The reasoning behind is that I think it's better to have an link at the end of the sentence
and not in the middle, so the reades doesn't need to finish the sentence to know what is it about and go back
and find the link. This way all the information is given before the link, so the users stops reading and can
click directly.

### Merge pull request #3686 from eksperimental/correct_raise

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>

Format raise message consistently
----

### Format raise message consistently

**Author:** eksperimental <eksperimental@users.noreply.github.com>
**Date:** 2015-09-08 00:23:14 +0700
**Commit:** a251ccc722a3736a5a5f79edcb69b5fb749b3738

Kernel.raise messages should not start with uppercase, and should not
have trailing punctuation.

Mix.raise messages should start with uppercase, and should not have a trailing
puntuation.

    # starting with uppercase
    # exclude (Mix.raise, and any message that starts with all uppecase such as: I or IO
    ag '(?<!Mix\.)raise\s+\"+(?!([A-Z]+\b))[A-Z]'

    # Mix.raise should start with upper case
    # except when `mix` or `rebar` are mentioned
    ag 'Mix\.raise\s+\"(?!(mix|rebar))+[a-z]'

    # ending in period
    ag "raise\s+\"+.+\.\"\s*\n"

**Date:** 2015-09-07 18:16:21 +0700
**Commit:** 8d5c009b13f01a23a7b396407d4541405e69432d

It makes it consistent with the rest of the docs.

I have just looked for the first line in @doc, @moduledoc, so lines bellow it may still need
to be corrected.

@shortdoc all have been corrected.

Inspired by: https://github.com/elixir-lang/elixir/pull/2942

Command to list the first lines:

```sh
  # first line in @moduledoc / @doc
  ag '(@(module)?doc)\s+("{3})(\r|\n|.)\s*+[\w-]+(?<!s)\b'

  # also @shortdoc
  ag '(@shortdoc)\s+("{1,3})(\r|\n|.)\s*+[\w-]+(?<!s)\b'
```

**Date:** 2015-09-07 02:09:21 +1000
**Commit:** eb9c7768c72ba5ce8a63d83d3e0176bd992bbea2

New version can parse "+digit" like "+1.0, and supports E as exponent markter as well as e. + after the exponent marker is legitimate too.
So, Float.parse("+1.0E+2x") returns {100.0, "x"} now.

fix(Kernel): check arguments on if and unless
----

### fix(Kernel): check arguments on if and unless

**Author:** Gary Rennie <gazler@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-08-24 23:41:11 +0100
**Commit:** c53da7ae12d7ce3ebbdceb047a1062369cffb20b

`Kernel.if` and `Kernel.unless` are now only valid if there is exactly
one `do` key and optionally an `else` key.

Any calls to the if macro that do not satisfy these conditions raise an
ArgumentError with a relevant error message.

This closes #3614

foo = """
this \"\"\"

### doctests raising exceptions and assert_raise show original stacktrace

**Author:** Abel Muiño <amuino@1uptalent.com>
**Date:** 2015-08-08 12:53:48 +0200
**Commit:** fe4c851424fefb09aae9ac09d9650a3d1a299f69

Currently doctests report the type and message of the raised exception,
but a synthetic stacktrace.
`assert_raise` does not provide any information about why
the wrong exception was raised, since the stacktrace points to test
line.

This change makes it display the original stacktrace too, which is more
useful for debugging and keeps symmetry with the information that
standard ExTests provide.

### Optimize many functions in String

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2015-08-02 13:49:43 +0200
**Commit:** 0e7232671b394637e3da0267c01594820f7917e6

Many functions relied on next_grapheme, which would traverse
the whole string generating a lot of graphemes as garbage along
the way.

This commit introduces next_grapheme_size, which returns the
size in bytes of the next grapheme, avoiding the generation
of the garbage and optimizing functions that need just the
byte_size.

Functions optimized are: String.at, String.split_at, String.slice
and String.length.

----

The docs for the Inspect protocol mentioned that if there's an error
while inspecting a struct (for which the Inspect protocol has been
implemented), then the struct is printed with a raw representation.

struct is printed raw in the error message.

----

### Streamline aliases and imports in lexical tracker

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2015-07-22 09:59:31 +0200
**Commit:** 80fd98f01afbf9118e6dc64b82441229da2f9909

Aliases should not mark nodes as compile or runtime
since it receives the alias to be expanded. The
expanded alias is always called later though as a
remote dispatch.

Imports should always mark nodes as compile time,
since they are compile time dependencies.

Finally, ensure compile nodes have higher priority
over runtime ones.

----

### Reduce noise in lexical tracker output

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2015-07-22 02:53:25 +0200
**Commit:** 1fd72ba72f94c96d56a308539184bffdaebdd723

### Only recompile compile time dependencies in mix projects

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2015-07-22 01:19:57 +0200
**Commit:** 7e0ecec061afcc0f437944daa4ef74b42cec935d

Prior to this patch, if module A invoked B, regardless of
where, A would recompile if B was changed.

Since the previous commit, we are now able to distinguish
between compile time dependencies (macros and calls outside
of a function) from runtime ones (everything else).

Now when traversing the dependency tree, runtime dependencies
since marks a given module as stale. If there is, however, a
compile time dependency on any stale module, the module that
depends on the stale dependency is recompiled.

To be more precise, imagine this flow:

    A [calls macro in] B [calls function in] C

Previously, A, B and C would recompile if C changed. Now only
A will.

----

### Favor explicitness in Mix.Utils.read_path with rebar/archive installs

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2015-07-18 21:27:15 +0200
**Commit:** 4ff286f209173b0320ac4d7709648d69e3f4b484

The previous code had a bunch of indirection and if/else
clauses because we tried to shoe horn all different concerns
from rebar, archives, reading and copying in a single place.

The new code has a simple Mix.Utils.read_path/2 which returns
the exact failure reasons to the calling code, allowing us to
provide a better easy experience regardless of the failure.

**Date:** 2015-06-22 14:43:22 +0200
**Commit:** a46681e1e5192f724560faa8e17a6303342ae464

perform the copying and consolidation for every command
executed, so we need to require an explicit compilation step
to avoid performing such tasks multiple times.

### Update Application docs to make them more accurate.

**Author:** Myron Marston <myron.marston@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-06-19 14:26:16 -0700
**Commit:** 73ea36b7c9384abf21b96c52f1b77467c9fbd3ab

The docs claimed that `get_env`/`fetch_env`/`fetch_env!` return
a default value/return :error/raise an exception when passed an
application that is not loaded. However, this is not the observed
behavior:

iex(1)> Application.put_env :foo, :bar, :baz
:ok
iex(2)> Application.get_env :foo, :bar
:baz

### Move terminate reason to second line in logs

**Author:** James Fish <james@fishcakez.com>
**Date:** 2015-06-19 23:53:12 +0100
**Commit:** 6cd1478a2ec1a3dad7f02fb5b9291abe3a3a427f

With default settings logger could truncate the log before the
terminate reason if there is a large term beforehand, such as a large
message in a GenServer termination report. Therefore move terminate
reason (the most important information) as near the front of the
message as possible so that as little of it as possible is truncated.

### Merge pull request #3356 from gabrielelana/surround-many-handles-empty

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-05-27 22:47:01 +0200
**Commit:** 4f68c4f10502e0f54a21093bb9a33957e63a9ac4

`surround_many/5` handles empty documents
----

### `surround_many/5` handles empty documents

**Author:** gabriele.lana <gabriele.lana@cleancode.it>
**Date:** 2015-05-27 06:56:10 +0200
**Commit:** feacf14a352ae19adba7a5352a7a23d732e69392

`surround/3` correctly handles empty documents, ex

    iex> doc = Inspect.Algebra.surround "[", Inspect.Algebra.empty, "]"
    iex> Inspect.Algebra.format(doc, 80) |> IO.iodata_to_binary
    "[]"

But `surround_many/5` didn't

    iex> fun = fn(d, _) -> d end
    iex> opts = %Inspect.Opts{}
    iex> what = ["a", Inspect.Algebra.empty]
    iex> doc = Inspect.Algebra.surround_many("[", what, "]", opts, fun, ",")
    iex> Inspect.Algebra.format(doc, 80) |> IO.iodata_to_binary
    "[a, ]"

This is what I expect and what it does now

    iex> doc = Inspect.Algebra.surround_many("[", what, "]", opts, fun, ",")
    iex> Inspect.Algebra.format(doc, 80) |> IO.iodata_to_binary
    "[a]"

### cli_test: skip test that relies on chmod() when under fakeroot

**Author:** Antonio Terceiro <terceiro@softwarelivre.org>
**Date:** 2015-05-05 18:50:05 -0300
**Commit:** b5a00d463d4ea328e0eff00b06115797861843b0

Debian packages are built using fakeroot(1), which is a wrapper that lets
subprocesses think they are being run as root even though they are not.
fakeroot will wrap chmod() and it won't to what you expect to.

### Makefile: allow passing REBAR and PREFIX via environment

**Author:** Antonio Terceiro <terceiro@softwarelivre.org>
**Date:** 2015-04-28 16:07:48 -0300
**Commit:** 1e037c42cd90b0d5a4c99612b13d3c1f300af40c

This allows trivial distro packaging by just settting REBAR and PREFIX
as environment variables in build scripts, and letting packaging helpers
handle make/make clean/make install on their own.

For building official Debian packages we can't have an embedded copy of
rebar which is already packaged on its own in Debian.

### Merge pull request #3302 from whatyouhide/mix-config-agent

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-05-03 11:36:54 +0200
**Commit:** 087c25b94417c4b1c42912c587c90296afec7423

Use agents to store Mix config
----

### Use agents to store Mix config

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-05-02 23:50:11 +0200
**Commit:** da1002257cca2841977eaf86de63851e36381619

Mix config was stored in a variable (`var!(config, Mix.Config)`) which was
initialized at every `use Mix.Config`. Using a variable worked well but didn't
allow pretty much anything outside "straight" code. For example, you could do
this:

    config :foo, bar: :baz

    if 3 + 2 == 5
      config :foo, [math: :check]
    end

but you could not do this:

    f = fn ->
      config :foo, bar: :baz
    end
    f.()

since functions can't leak their scope into the outside scope, so changes to the
`config` variable were not "persisted". `for` comprehensions were not allowed in
the same way.

This commit introduces a `Mix.Config.Agent` module so that configuration can be
stored inside an agent. An agent is started every time that `use Mix.Config` is
called, and is stopped by `Mix.Config.read!/1` (which is the basic unit for
reading configuration; everything else should use it).

### Fix inferred argument names in `Module.simplify_signature/2`

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-04-28 16:33:17 +0200
**Commit:** 34af7baf2df87306cd747a3f06bd091a62a42f17

Arguments like literals (e.g., `4`) were converted into variables like `int1`.
This commit changes this behaviour by adding an index only if necessary (i.e.,
there are duplicates in the signature). For example:

    def foo([], 1, %{}, 2)

becomes

    def foo(list, int1, map, int2)

### Implement `Tuple.append/2`

**Author:** Slava Pavlutin <sl.pavlutin@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-04-10 19:42:53 +0300
**Commit:** 30da6d26d568e945ff8ce3f5fbbfeeff8a8a561b

`Tuple.append/2` adds an element to the end of a tuple, which
mirrors what Erlang's `append_element/2` does. Therefore,
`Tuple.append/2` is implemented to maintain consistency of the API.

### Merge pull request #3263 from carlosantoniodasilva/ca-file-refactor

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-04-10 21:13:25 +0200
**Commit:** d5b793c73eb5e27cee134feb2d9fe5e22356b623

Refactor File! methods to avoid going through IO.chardata_to_string twice
----

### Refactor File! methods to avoid going through IO.chardata_to_string twice

**Author:** Carlos Antonio da Silva <carlosantoniodasilva@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-04-10 15:33:30 -0300
**Commit:** 11724d3b11485dff0fd38e2951a8adfb638ead44

All the non-! methods pass the pass argument through IO.chardata_to_string,
and we are doing this work twice for the ! methods since we convert once
before delegating to the non-! versions.

This moves the conversion to the exception case only, which should be
far less common, thus avoiding going through the duplicated call.

### No longer translate arguments to `@on_definition`

**Author:** Or Neeman <oneeman@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-04-04 16:05:12 -0600
**Commit:** ef8dc3c05245d2cdfdb241cfabd36e39fcc39ecf

Breaking change: the arguments to the `@on_definition` hook are no
longer expanded and translated, but are passed in their original quoted
form.  The only exception is module attributes (e.g. `@my_constant`),
which are expanded.

The reason for this change is that this hook is used for generating
functions' documentation, and the previous behavior was causing
incorrect signatures in the documentation.  See #3143 for details.

Closes #3143

Support specs for unreachable private functions
----

### Support specs for unreachable private functions

**Author:** Or Neeman <oneeman@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-03-29 19:44:06 -0600
**Commit:** babe8f7364fc83c3a05c48114006fd865e458a63

Unreachable private functions are excluded during
compilation. As a result, if such a function had a `@spec`,
it would cause a `CompileError`. This commit fixes that by
also excluding any specs for unreachable private functions.

Closes #3144

### EEx: Add trim option

**Author:** Or Neeman <oneeman@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-03-22 18:18:41 -0600
**Commit:** d90f6ed85870a52f5e143f82888fabb865670452

If the `trim` option is used, trims around EEx tags:
- On left, up to the line break if all whitespace
- On right, up to and including the line break if all whitespace

Does not trim around quotations (`<%% %>`), but does trim around
any tags inside a quotation.

Closes #3154

Fix doctest :only, so it accepts multiple functions
----

### Fix doctest :only, so it accepts multiple functions

**Author:** Martin Svalin <martin@lite.nu>
**Date:** 2015-03-15 02:09:03 +0100
**Commit:** 469017b6c1de40d36343a1664ab98ebd26dfea57

There is a bug in `doctest` with the `:only` option, such that it only works if
the list is of size 1. If you pass two functions to the option, `filter_by_opts`
will always be empty, since the function is checked with `Enum.all?`.

`Enum.any?` returns false for an empty list. Thus the extra `Enum.empty?` check.

### Revert "Merge pull request #3109 from oneeman/refine_no_parens_parsing"

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2015-03-02 11:14:12 +0100
**Commit:** 4ecc9ee1dfc375294a4f61aaa58f4fed35e5d476

This pull request has broken the following expression:

    assert [] = TestRepo.all from p in Post, where: p.title in ^[]

Previously it was parsed as:

    assert [] = TestRepo.all(from p in Post, where: p.title in ^[])

But after this pull request it is parsed as:

    assert [] = TestRepo.all from(p in Post), where: p.title in ^[]

This reverts commit 7f528aa1c035bf5f0e62fe20b6e3975c0084f395, reversing
changes made to 98746e08eaa2bf58c202e8500b6cf83ed2368cc0.

Conflicts:
	lib/elixir/src/elixir_parser.yrl

- Fix tokenization of `a@b: ` and `A!: `

----

### Update docs for `IEx.Helpers.import_file/1`

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-02-11 12:48:10 +0100
**Commit:** 165c6b0792547cf27ddca2c33ccd240c0229c623

I updated the docs for the `IEx.Helpers.import_file/1` macro in order to reflect
the introduction of the `:optional` option.

----

### Add an :optional option to IEx.Helpers.import_file/1

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-02-11 12:37:46 +0100
**Commit:** ca4161146be3e1e8201b1d66fdbc5f5453457b3c

Added an `:optional` option to the `IEx.Helpers.import_file/1` macro which tells
the macro not to raise exceptions if the given file is missing. This is useful
when one wants to import a file but isn't sure that the file exists (a common
use case is to check for `~/.iex.exs` in a local `.iex.exs`):

    import_file "~/.iex.exs", optional: true

----

### Add tests for IEx.Helpers.import_file/1 with missing file

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <an.leopardi@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2015-02-11 12:35:32 +0100
**Commit:** 95809e06c3904f60b53b8e4fb65e8afb8135450b

Added (failing) tests to show how the `IEx.Helpers.import_file/1` macro behaves
when the given file is missing. These tests are failing since they test the
future behaviour of `import_file/1` which will accept an `:optional` option.

### Logger: Format OTP 17.4 terminate reasons

**Author:** James Fish <james@fishcakez.com>
**Date:** 2015-02-10 20:12:43 +0000
**Commit:** 897359a5238736d989a6989eb6e0b392ecb6aea6

From 17.4 OTP behaviours include the stacktrace in the logged reason
when exit/1 is called. If an elixir exception is raised, or the reason
can be normalized to an elixir exception (not ErlangError), the
exception is formatted as an exception:
** (Exception) message
Otherwise the reason is formatted using Exception.format_exit/1:
** (stop) :reason

**Author:** Andrea Leopardi <whatyouhide@andreas-rmbp.local>
**Date:** 2015-02-03 17:00:11 +0100
**Commit:** 478215d60ffc7ed27f2efb4786e9ecb349ab1849

When `raise/1` (or `reraise/2`) is called with an invalid argument (not a module
name neither a binary message), a cryptic `CaseClauseError` is raised. This
behaviour exposes the underlying implementation details of the `raise/1` (or
`reraise/2`) macro.

### Introduce an implementation of Integer.digits/1

**Author:** Matthew Rothenberg <mrothenberg@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2014-12-01 20:05:54 -0500
**Commit:** 825fa7ac4d3d7d156058c1f1b1b02e4a59798de0

Introduces an efficient (purely mathematical) implementation of
Integer.digits/1.

Most developers tend to implement the digits of an integer by converting
to a string representation, splitting the string, and then converting
each char back into an integer.  E.g. with code such as the following:

```elixir
def digits(n) when is_integer(n) and n>0 do
    n
    |> Integer.to_string
    |> String.graphemes
    |> Enum.map(&String.to_integer/1)
end
```
Which results in a lot of inefficient type conversion and memory
allocation.

This implementation is purely mathematical.  In informal benchmarks on
my workstation, it performs at 0.9 μs/op versus 4.8 μs/op in the naïve
implementation above.

----

### Ensure compilation does not leave random spurious data on failure

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2014-11-21 17:40:04 +0100
**Commit:** 2935e7149a7ae98ca5cbf0908a132e0c0431d662

Prior to this patch, the parallel compiler could leave spurious
processes and the module definition could leave stale data
in the elixir_modules table.

The issue has been fixed by emitting the proper exit signals
and by using monitors.

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2014-11-07 10:56:31 -0300
**Commit:** 7eff42ef5bf06b286a3c539a6c394ac8bbdb50a4

Make IO.ANSI.Docs readable on white background
----

### Make IO.ANSI.Docs readable on white background

**Author:** Martin Frost <frost@ceri.se>
**Date:** 2014-11-03 15:33:12 +0100
**Commit:** df44ad4fb2f8caf607e00d05147947d23ed8e1a3

The `default_options` for `IO.ANSI.Docs` uses bright yellow for
documentation headers and titles.

This is very nice and readable when using a terminal with a dark
background. However, when using a terminal with a bright (e.g. white)
background, those parts are unfortunately illegible.

This patch removes the `:bright` part from the `default_options`, to
make the defaults better suited to more people.

----

### Use unamed tables for module compilation

**Author:** José Valim <jose.valim@plataformatec.com.br>
**Date:** 2014-10-17 12:02:26 +0200
**Commit:** 8de8a56223fc22d916071d47530079cd257de3c7

* We have created a main named table that keeps all module information

* We have reduced the amount of tables by storing internal, docs and
  attributes altogether and using table looks for retriving the relevant
  information

* At this moment, creating a module in Elixir defines three tables,
  one for data, another for functions and another for clauses

### Revert "Based on email from Paulo Almeida, I removed the apparently extranous calls"

**Author:** Booker C. Bense <bbense@gmail.com>
**Date:** 2014-10-10 16:43:17 -0700
**Commit:** 7f6fb27fbdffd091624fd85024a1d53cbd18fbef

This reverts commit cd5ed1db59d1f93197fef72311e59cb42ed2ebc4.

Based on looking in the rest of file.ex and the foo and foo! methods,
they all use this same pattern. It's really kind of moot since
IO.char_to_string is almost always a single if_string return.

Either they should all be fixed, or they should all stay the same.

It might make sense to write a generalized bang macro.
